# Balance run nov6-v606-deep

Build 0e4119d7, deep size, 10-card deck, 78 min this session, settings {"CONTESTS":"byColor"}.
Games: Normal 1800 at 4 players, 300 at 3, 300 at 2; Hard 240 at 4; Very Hard 80 at 4; one Hard seat among Normals 120.

## Findings
(Written by the feel step: at most 10, most important first, each with the card's full text, one played-out turn, and which sources agree.)

## Trust: SUSPECT
Verdict: SUSPECT.
A stronger bot (Hard) in one seat against three Normal bots won 53% of 120 games (even 25%, give or take 5), ending 12.7 followers ahead of the average Normal. The closer to even, the closer Normal plays to strong play, and the more Normal-only numbers can be trusted (under 12 points over even: trusted; over 25: suspect).
Normal and Hard on the same members (70 members with 30+ Hard owners): of the 27 that stand out under either, 18 point the same way; overall the two levels' reads move together by 0.33 (1 = the same order, 0 = unrelated).
The levels disagree on: Patron Saints (silver) (Normal 5, Hard -1); Cheerleaders (benders) (Normal 5, Hard -1); Brokers (trading) (Normal 5, Hard -4); Regular (market) (Normal -3, Hard 3); Copyist (echo) (Normal -4, Hard 4); Guest of Honor (silver) (Normal -5, Hard 3); Doppelganger (echo) (Normal -5, Hard 1); Party Crasher (benders) (Normal -5, Hard 2); Big Tent (yellow) (Normal -7, Hard 11). Findings on these need Hard.

## Flags for the feel step to check
- Abuse: Ballroom Dancers is worth clearly more than its price to a player trying to break it.
- Abuse: Cheerleaders is worth clearly more than its price to a player trying to break it.
- Card class: **DOMINANT**: Foster Parents (red) (N +3 @94%, H +11 @95%, V 0 @97%)
- Card class: **TRAP**: Copyist (echo) (N -4 @67%, H +4 @57%); Doppelganger (echo) (N -5 @53%, H +1 @56%); Impersonator (echo) (N -5 @62%, H -7 @56%)

# Choice report

32059 looked-ahead decisions from 240 games (.balance-runs/nov6-v606-deep/hard4-0.jsonl.choices, .balance-runs/nov6-v606-deep/hard4-1.jsonl.choices, .balance-runs/nov6-v606-deep/hard4-2.jsonl.choices, .balance-runs/nov6-v606-deep/hard4-3.jsonl.choices, .balance-runs/nov6-v606-deep/hard4-4.jsonl.choices, .balance-runs/nov6-v606-deep/hard4-5.jsonl.choices, .balance-runs/nov6-v606-deep/hard4-6.jsonl.choices, .balance-runs/nov6-v606-deep/hard4-7.jsonl.choices, .balance-runs/nov6-v606-deep/veryhard4-0.jsonl.choices, .balance-runs/nov6-v606-deep/veryhard4-1.jsonl.choices, .balance-runs/nov6-v606-deep/veryhard4-2.jsonl.choices, .balance-runs/nov6-v606-deep/veryhard4-3.jsonl.choices, .balance-runs/nov6-v606-deep/veryhard4-4.jsonl.choices, .balance-runs/nov6-v606-deep/veryhard4-5.jsonl.choices, .balance-runs/nov6-v606-deep/veryhard4-6.jsonl.choices, .balance-runs/nov6-v606-deep/veryhard4-7.jsonl.choices). 19% of all decisions were close calls.

How to read: for every decision the strong bot plays out a few options (its contenders) and keeps the one that ends best, in followers of margin. A **close call** is a decision where it and at least one other contender ended within 1.5 followers of the best: a real dilemma. An **auto-pick** is taken more than 5 followers ahead of the runner-up, or as the only contender while 3+ options were legal. **Solo** picks had no other contender: Normal's quick score ruled the rest out, so an auto-pick that is mostly solo is Normal's judgment, not the lookahead's. **Thin** picks came from a menu at most 50% of the usual size: taken because little else was left. **Regret** is how far behind the pick it ended when played out and passed over (small = nearly as good). **Disagree** is how often the cheap Normal bot's top pick and the lookahead's top pick split over this card; "under" means the lookahead liked it best and Normal didn't, "over" the reverse. High disagreement says Normal-bot numbers on it are suspect, not the card. Round columns read offered/chosen/close. Tags (offered >= 20): REAL CHOICE close >= 10%; AUTO-PICK auto >= 50% of picks and chosen >= 10%; FILLER thin >= 50% of picks and close < 5%; DEAD contender < 10% and chosen < 3%; MISJUDGED disagree >= 50% of 8+ scored appearances; MIXED none of these. Means from short rollouts are noisy: read a tag as a lead, not a verdict.

## Catch-up and runaway leaders (Normal bots)
- 2 players, 240 games (even chance 50%): the leader after round I won 62%, the last after round I won 38%; the leader after round II won 66%, the last after round II won 34%.
  A winner 10+ followers behind going into round III: 5% of games. Lead after round II averaged 9.2; final winning margin 18.2.
- 3 players, 240 games (even chance 33%): the leader after round I won 39%, the last after round I won 30%; the leader after round II won 55%, the last after round II won 18%.
  A winner 10+ followers behind going into round III: 11% of games. Lead after round II averaged 6.3; final winning margin 12.1.
- 4 players, 1440 games (even chance 25%): the leader after round I won 34%, the last after round I won 20%; the leader after round II won 47%, the last after round II won 12%.
  A winner 10+ followers behind going into round III: 16% of games. Lead after round II averaged 6.3; final winning margin 10.9.

## Catch-up and runaway leaders (Hard bots)
- 4 players, 192 games (even chance 25%): the leader after round I won 35%, the last after round I won 21%; the leader after round II won 48%, the last after round II won 13%.
  A winner 10+ followers behind going into round III: 21% of games. Lead after round II averaged 6.6; final winning margin 8.4.

## Interaction and game length (Normal bots)
- 2 players, 300 games: 5.5 moves a game take from a rival (resources 2.8, followers 1.1, member 1.6); 100% of them hit the leader (even 100%); 7.9 gifts to rivals.
  Kingmaking: 0.55 times a game, a player 15+ behind in round III hit one of the top two.
  Length: 24 turns and 115 decisions a game (4.7 per turn).
- 3 players, 300 games: 11.7 moves a game take from a rival (resources 6.3, followers 2.2, member 3.2); 57% of them hit the leader (even 50%); 20.0 gifts to rivals.
  Kingmaking: 1.28 times a game, a player 15+ behind in round III hit one of the top two.
  Length: 37 turns and 182 decisions a game (5.0 per turn).
- 4 players, 1800 games: 17.6 moves a game take from a rival (resources 9.1, member 5.5, followers 3.0); 43% of them hit the leader (even 33%); 34.7 gifts to rivals.
  Kingmaking: 2.28 times a game, a player 15+ behind in round III hit one of the top two.
  Length: 49 turns and 247 decisions a game (5.1 per turn).

## Interaction and game length (Hard bots)
- 4 players, 240 games: 18.3 moves a game take from a rival (resources 9.7, member 5.7, followers 2.9); 42% of them hit the leader (even 33%); 34.0 gifts to rivals.
  Kingmaking: 2.67 times a game, a player 15+ behind in round III hit one of the top two.
  Length: 49 turns and 248 decisions a game (5.1 per turn).

## How winners won (Normal bots, 1920 games)
About 7.4 equally common winning plans. The most common:
- mixed colors, wide (9+ members): 22%
- mostly red, wide (9+ members): 17%
- mostly market, wide (9+ members): 14%
- mixed colors, few members (5 or less): 11%
- mixed colors, 6 to 8 members: 11%
- mostly purple, wide (9+ members): 9%
- mostly echo, wide (9+ members): 5%
- mostly silver, wide (9+ members): 4%

## How winners won (Hard bots, 192 games)
About 7.0 equally common winning plans. The most common:
- mostly red, wide (9+ members): 20%
- mixed colors, wide (9+ members): 19%
- mixed colors, few members (5 or less): 18%
- mostly market, wide (9+ members): 13%
- mixed colors, 6 to 8 members: 9%
- mostly purple, wide (9+ members): 8%
- mostly silver, wide (9+ members): 4%
- mostly echo, wide (9+ members): 4%

## Focus (Normal bots): colors among a cult's members (grey left out)
- Every player: 2.6 colors, 58% of members in their biggest color.
- Winners: 2.6 colors, 58% of members in their biggest color.

## Focus (Hard bots): colors among a cult's members (grey left out)
- Every player: 2.7 colors, 57% of members in their biggest color.
- Winners: 2.7 colors, 58% of members in their biggest color.

## Skill by color (main color's win share above even, Normal bots then Hard bots)
A color that does better under Hard rewards good play; one that drops plays itself or only beats weak players.
- silver: +5 → +18 points (229 and 20 seats)
- purple: +7 → +15 points (388 and 41 seats)
- red: +16 → +19 points (645 and 86 seats)
- market: +7 → +10 points (609 and 72 seats)
- thieves: -8 → -11 points (46 and 7 seats)
- echo: +15 → +10 points (156 and 27 seats)
- trading: -2 → -8 points (167 and 24 seats)
- yellow: -5 → -12 points (123 and 16 seats)

## Abuse hunt
### Abuser test: Anarchist

"On Use, once per round: swap any 2 members, in any cult or the shop, that cost within 2 of each other." (costs 7; 10-card deck; 4 players; rivals normal; 8 seeds, 7 min)

With the card, the abuser ended +0.4 followers against the best rival compared with the same game without it (give or take 6.3; bought in 8 of 8 games, after paying 7).
It won 8 of 8 with the card and 8 of 8 without. (Games with and without the card go different ways after the first change, so this number swings a lot on few games.)
The card itself gave the abuser 0.1 followers on average (printed, during play and at game end; per game 1, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0, 0) for a price of 7: -6.9 against spending the same on follower buys.
Not flagged.

Best game (seed 5001, table red, yellow, thieves, market, bought in round 1): 14.0 followers better. What the card did:
- P2 uses Anarchist.
- P2's Anarchist swapped P2's Accomplice and Shop's Night Traders.
- P2 recruited Anarchist (1 red, 5 yellow, 1 blue).
- P2's Anarchist swapped P2's Anarchist and Shop's Victors.
- New in the shop: Anarchist.
- P4 recruited Anarchist (3 red, 1 yellow, 2 magenta, 1 blue).
- P4 uses Anarchist.
- P4's Anarchist swapped P3's Dyer and P4's Anarchist.
- P3 uses Anarchist.
- P3's Anarchist swapped P3's Window Dressers and Shop's Accomplice.
- Churn: P1 sends Anarchist to the bottom of the pile.
